\widetilde{M}_{s,c}:=\widetilde{M}_{n-1}\widetilde{M}_{n-2}\ldots\widetilde{M}_{0}
. It is easy to see that
\widetilde{M}_{s,c}
can be implemented efficiently given
(s,c)
. The rest of the proof of the claim is therefore dedicated to proving correctness of the construction.

We now show that the above construction satisfies Claim
7.1
by a series of subclaims. First we note that with high probability,
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)
is close to
\widetilde{p}_{1|z}
.

SubClaim 7.1
.

For all
i\in[0,n-1]
, for all
z\in\{0,1\}^{i}

\Prr\left[\left|{\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)-\widetilde{p}_{1|z}\right|\geq\frac{n}{\sqrt{p(n)}}\right]\leq 2e^{-2n^{2}}

Proof.

Follows from the definition of
\widetilde{p}_{1|z}
and setting
\delta
to be
n
in the additive Chernoff bound (Theorem
4.1
).
∎

Next we note that since
E_{i},P,
and
(E_{i})^{\dagger}
do not affect the computational basis state on the
\mathsf{Z}
register, for all
s,c
, for all
i\in[0,n-1]
, for all
z\in\{0,1\}^{i}
, there exists a state
|\sigma_{z}\rangle
such that

\left(E_{i}\right)^{\dagger}PE_{i}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}=|z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|\sigma_{z}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}\mathsf{V}\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

The next subclaim shows that we can synthesize a state close to
|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle
.

SubClaim 7.2
.

For all
i\in[0,n-1]
, for all
z\in\{0,1\}^{i}
, if
n
is large enough

\left||\sigma_{z}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}\mathsf{V}\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}-|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}\right|\leq\sqrt{3n}/p(n)^{1/4}

Proof.

First we apply
\left(E_{i}\right)^{\dagger}PE_{i}
to both terms and note this does not change the absolute value of their difference.

\displaystyle\left||\sigma_{z}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}\mathsf{V}\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}-|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left||z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|\sigma_{z}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}\mathsf{V}\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}-|z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left(E_{i}\right)^{\dagger}PE_{i}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}-|z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|PE_{i}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}-E_{i}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}\right|^{2}

(7)

Next, we use the definition of
E_{i}
and
P
to expand each term.
Expanding
E_{i}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle E_{i}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=|z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}\sum_{v}\sqrt{\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]}|v\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\mathsf{junk}_{v}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

(8)

Expanding
PE_{i}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle PE_{i}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=P|z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}\sum_{v}\sqrt{\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}|v\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\mathsf{junk}_{v}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=|z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}\sum_{v}\sqrt{\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]}|\psi_{v}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}|v\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\mathsf{junk}_{v}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

(9)

Plugging (
8
) and (
9
) into (
7
) gives

\displaystyle\left||\sigma_{z}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}\mathsf{V}\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}-|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left||z\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}\sum_{v}\sqrt{\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]}\left(|\psi_{v}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}-|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}\right)|v\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\mathsf{junk}_{v}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{v}\sqrt{\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]}\left(|\psi_{v}\rangle-|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle\right)|v\rangle\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{v}\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]\left||\psi_{v}\rangle-|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle\right|^{2}

(10)

SubClaim
7.1
shows that all but a negligible fraction of the probability mass in the output of
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
is on
v
that are
n/\sqrt{p(n)}
close to
\widetilde{p}_{1|z}
. We can therefore bound the contribution
v
values that are not close, i.e. for
{\mathbb{V}}:=\left\{v:\left|v-\widetilde{p}_{1|z}\right|\leq\frac{n}{\sqrt{p(n)}}\right\}
, we see that terms not in
{\mathbb{V}}
contribute negligible amounts to the sum.

\displaystyle\sum_{v}\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]\left||\psi_{v}\rangle-|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in{\mathbb{V}}}\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]\left||\psi_{v}\rangle-|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle\right|^{2}+\sum_{v\notin{\mathbb{V}}}\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]\left||\psi_{v}\rangle-|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in{\mathbb{V}}}\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]\left||\psi_{v}\rangle-|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle\right|^{2}+4\sum_{v\notin{\mathbb{V}}}\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in{\mathbb{V}}}\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]\left||\psi_{v}\rangle-|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle\right|^{2}+8e^{-2n^{2}}

(11)

where the fourth step uses SubClaim
7.1
to show that
\sum_{v\notin{\mathbb{V}}}\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]
. Now, for any
v\in{\mathbb{V}}
, since
v
and
\widetilde{p}_{1|z}
are close, we can bound the first term. By definition,
|\psi_{v}\rangle=\sqrt{v}|1\rangle+\sqrt{1-v}|0\rangle
and
|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle=\sqrt{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}|1\rangle+\sqrt{1-\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}|0\rangle
. Therefore, for
v\notin{\mathbb{V}}

\displaystyle\left||\psi_{v}\rangle-|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sqrt{v}|1\rangle+\sqrt{1-v}|0\rangle-\sqrt{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}|1\rangle-\sqrt{1-\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}|0\rangle\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{v}-\sqrt{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\right)^{2}+\left(\sqrt{1-v}-\sqrt{1-\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\left(\sqrt{v}-\sqrt{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\right)\left(\sqrt{v}+\sqrt{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\right)\right|+\left|\left(\sqrt{1-v}-\sqrt{1-\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\right)\left(\sqrt{1-v}+\sqrt{1-\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\left|v-\widetilde{p}_{1|z}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2n/\sqrt{p(n)}

where the last step follows directly from the definition of
{\mathbb{V}}
. Substituting this bound in (
11
) and plugging the result into (
10
) gives

\displaystyle\left||\sigma_{z}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}\mathsf{V}\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}-|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}^{\prime}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in{\mathbb{V}}}\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]\left||\psi_{v}\rangle-|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle\right|^{2}+8e^{-2n^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in{\mathbb{V}}}\Prr[v={\mathcal{E}}_{i}(z)]\cdot 2n/\sqrt{p(n)}+8e^{-2n^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2n/\sqrt{p(n)}+8e^{-2n^{2}}\leq 3n/\sqrt{p(n)}

where the last step uses the fact that
n
is large enough. This concludes the proof of SubClaim
7.2
.
∎

For all
i\in[0,n-1]
, define the unitary
M_{i}
that for all
z\in\{0,1\}^{i}
maps
|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i+1}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{A}_{i}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}_{i^{\prime}}}
to
|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i}}|\psi_{p_{1|z}}\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i+1}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{A}_{i}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}_{i^{\prime}}}
. These represent steps in the amplitude step of Aaronson synthesis. Define
|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}:=\bigotimes_{j}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{j}}
. Note that when
z
is the empty string,
p_{b|z}=p_{b}
.

SubClaim 7.3
.

For all
i\in[0,n-1]

M_{i}M_{i-1}\ldots M_{1}M_{0}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i+1}\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}=\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i+1}}\sqrt{p_{z}}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i+1}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}

Proof.

We prove by induction on
i
. Consider the base case when
i=0
. By definition,
|\psi_{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\rangle=\sqrt{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}|1\rangle+\sqrt{1-\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}|0\rangle
, so

\displaystyle M_{0}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=|\psi_{p_{1|z}}\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left(\sqrt{p_{0}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1}}+\sqrt{p_{1}}|1\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1}}\right)|0\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}}\sqrt{p_{z}}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}

Suppose the SubClaim holds for all
i<i^{\prime}
. Then by applying the induction hypothesis

\displaystyle M_{i^{\prime}}M_{i^{\prime}-1}\ldots M_{1}M_{0}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i^{\prime}+1}\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=M_{i^{\prime}}\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i^{\prime}}}\sqrt{p_{z}}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i^{\prime}+1}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i^{\prime}}}\sqrt{p_{z}}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i^{\prime}}}\left(\sqrt{p_{0|z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i^{\prime}+1}}+\sqrt{p_{1|z}}|1\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i^{\prime}+1}}\right)|0\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i^{\prime}+1}}\sqrt{p_{z}}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i^{\prime}+1}}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i^{\prime}+1}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}

Which concludes the proof of SubClaim
7.3
.
∎

Next we show via hybrid argument that
M_{i}
can be replaced by
\widetilde{M}_{i}
one by one for each
i
.
Let

\Delta_{s,c,i}:=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i}}p_{z}\left(\left|p_{0|z}-\widetilde{p}_{0|z}\right|+\left|p_{1|z}-\widetilde{p}_{1|z}\right|\right)

Note that by the definition of
\Delta_{s,c}
, the contribution to
\Delta_{s,c}
in the case when
b_{\text{mode}}=0
may be written as the expectation over
i
of
\Delta_{s,c,i}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\Delta_{s,c}

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{Pr}[b_{\text{mode}}=0]\cdot\sum_{i}\mathrm{Pr}[i]\cdot\Delta_{s,c,i}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i}\Delta_{s,c,i}/2n

SubClaim 7.4
.

For all
i\in[0,n]
, define

|\cent_{i}\rangle:=\widetilde{M}_{n-1}\widetilde{M}_{n-2}\ldots\widetilde{M}_{i}M_{i-1}\ldots M_{0}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}

Then for all
i\in[0,n-1]

\left||\cent_{i+1}\rangle-|\cent_{i}\rangle\right|\leq\sqrt{3n}/p(n)^{1/4}+\sqrt{2\Delta_{s,c,i}}

Proof.

By expanding the definitions,

\displaystyle\left||\cent_{i+1}\rangle-|\cent_{i}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\widetilde{M}_{n-1}\widetilde{M}_{n-2}\ldots\widetilde{M}_{i+1}\left(M_{i}-\widetilde{M}_{i}\right)M_{i-1}\ldots M_{0}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left(M_{i}-\widetilde{M}_{i}\right)M_{i-1}\ldots M_{0}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\right|

Applying SubClaim
7.3
to
M_{i-1}\ldots M_{0}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}
we get

\displaystyle\left||\cent_{i+1}\rangle-|\cent_{i}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left(M_{i}-\widetilde{M}_{i}\right)\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i}}\sqrt{p_{z}}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i+1\ldots n}\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\right|

Expanding
M_{i}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i+1\ldots n}\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}

M_{i}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i+1\ldots n}\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}=|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i}}|\psi_{{p_{1|z}}}\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i+1}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i+2\ldots n}\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}

Expanding
\widetilde{M}_{i}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i+1\ldots n}\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{M}_{i}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i+1\ldots n}\mathsf{A}}|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{M}_{i}|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i+1\ldots n}\mathsf{A}}\left(|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{1}}\ldots|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=|z\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{1\ldots i}}|\sigma_{z}\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i+1}\mathsf{A}_{i}\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{i}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{X}_{i+2\ldots n}\mathsf{A}_{1\ldots i-1,i+1\ldots n}}\left(|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{1}}\ldots|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{i}-1}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{i}+1}\ldots|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{n}}\right)

When taking the norm of the difference, the registers
\mathsf{X}_{i+2\ldots n},\mathsf{A}_{1\ldots i-1,i+1\ldots n},\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{1\ldots i-1,i+1\ldots n}
are identical in both states and may be ignored. Therefore by the triangle inequality

\displaystyle\left||\cent_{i+1}\rangle-|\cent_{i}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i}}\sqrt{p_{z}}|z\rangle\left(|\psi_{{p_{1|z}}}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle-|\sigma_{z}\rangle\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i}}\sqrt{p_{z}}|z\rangle\left(|\psi_{{p_{1|z}}}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle-|\psi_{{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle+|\psi_{{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle-|\sigma_{z}\rangle\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i}}\sqrt{p_{z}}|z\rangle\left(|\psi_{{p_{1|z}}}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle-|\psi_{{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle\right)\right|+\left|\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i}}\sqrt{p_{z}}|z\rangle\left(|\psi_{{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle-|\sigma_{z}\rangle\right)\right|

Consider the first term. Intuitively,
\widetilde{p}_{1|z}
is on average
\Delta_{s,c,i}
far from
p_{1|z}
(over randomness of
z
). We can therefore bound the first term as follows.

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i}}\sqrt{p_{z}}|z\rangle\left(|\psi_{{p_{1|z}}}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle-|\psi_{{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i}}p_{z}\left||\psi_{{p_{1|z}}}\rangle-|\psi_{{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}}\rangle\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i}}p_{z}\left|\left(\sqrt{p_{1|z}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\right)|1\rangle+\left(\sqrt{p_{0|z}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{p}_{0|z}}\right)|0\rangle\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i}}p_{z}\left(\left(\sqrt{p_{1|z}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}\right)^{2}+\left(\sqrt{p_{0|z}}-\sqrt{\widetilde{p}_{0|z}}\right)^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i}}p_{z}\left(\left|p_{1|z}-\widetilde{p}_{1|z}\right|+\left|p_{0|z}-\widetilde{p}_{0|z}\right|\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2\Delta_{s,c,i}}

Now, consider the second term. We have shown in SubClaim
7.2
that we synthesize a state close to
|\psi_{{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}}\rangle
, therefore

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i}}\sqrt{p_{z}}|z\rangle\left(|\psi_{{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle-|\sigma_{z}\rangle\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i}}p_{z}\left||\psi_{{\widetilde{p}_{1|z}}}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle-|\sigma_{z}\rangle\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{i}}p_{z}\cdot 3n/\sqrt{p(n)}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{3n}/p(n)^{1/4}

Where the third step follows from SubClaim
7.2
. Adding both error terms gives the final error and concludes the proof of SubClaim
7.4
.
∎

Now, summing up the errors from SubClaim
7.4

\displaystyle\left||\cent_{n}\rangle-|\cent_{0}\rangle\right|\leq\sum_{i\in[0,n-1]}\left||\cent_{i+1}\rangle-|\cent_{i}\rangle\right|\leq\left(n\cdot\sqrt{3n}/p(n)^{1/4}+\sum_{i}\sqrt{2\Delta_{s,c,i}}\right)

Applying Jensen’s inequality to the final term, followed by the definition of
\Delta_{s,c,i}

\displaystyle\left||\cent_{n}\rangle-|\cent_{0}\rangle\right|\leq\left(n\cdot\sqrt{3n}/p(n)^{1/4}+\sqrt{2n\sum_{i}\Delta_{s,c,i}}\right)\leq\sqrt{3n^{3}}/p(n)^{1/4}+2\sqrt{n\Delta_{s,c}}

Finally, we note that by SubClaim
7.3
,

|\cent_{n}\rangle=\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\sqrt{p_{z}}|z\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle=|\$^{*}_{s,c}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle

and by definition of
\widetilde{M}_{s,c}
and
|\cent_{0}\rangle

|\cent_{0}\rangle=\widetilde{M}_{s,c}|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle

Note that
|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle=|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{2}}\rangle
. Therefore

\left|\widetilde{M}_{s,c}|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle-|\$^{*}_{s,c}\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle\right|\leq\sqrt{3n^{3}}/p(n)^{1/4}+2\sqrt{n\Delta_{s,c}}

and therefore
which concludes the proof of Claim
7.1
.∎

Claim
7.1
shows how to construct
\widetilde{M}_{s,c}
for each
(s,c)
. We now construct
\widetilde{M}
that takes
(s,c)
as input and applies
\widetilde{M}_{s,c}
.

Claim 7.2
.

Let
|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle:=|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{}}\rangle^{\otimes np(n)}
. Then there exists an efficient unitary
\widetilde{M}
such that for all
s\in\{0,1\}^{n}
and
c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n}

\left|\widetilde{M}|s,c\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle-|s,c\rangle|\$^{*}_{s,c}\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle\right|\leq\sqrt{3n^{3}}/p(n)^{1/4}+2\sqrt{n\Delta_{s,c}}

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{M}
be defined as
\sum_{s,c}|s,c\rangle\langle s,c|\otimes\widetilde{M}_{s,c}
. The statement then follows directly from Claim
7.1
.
∎

Claim
7.2
shows how the adversary may be used to synthesize a state close to
|\$^{*}_{s,c}\rangle
. The next step in Aaronson’s synthesis is to coherently apply a phase to each basis vector
|z\rangle
in
|\$^{*}_{s,c}\rangle
to obtain
|\$_{s,c}\rangle
. We will show how to use the adversary to perform a similar task. Finally we will apply
\mathsf{c}^{\dagger}
to obtain an approximation to
|\$_{s}\rangle
.

Claim 7.3

(State Synthesis)
.

Let
|\tau_{\mathsf{synth}}\rangle:=|\tau_{\mathsf{}}\rangle^{\otimes(2n+1)p(n)}
. There exists an efficient algorithm
{\mathcal{B}}
such that

\displaystyle\underset{s,|\$_{s}\rangle\leftarrow{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})}{{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\langle\$_{s}|{\mathcal{B}}(s,|\tau_{\mathsf{synth}}\rangle)|\$_{s}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-1/q(n)

Proof.

We first construct an algorithm that takes input
z^{\prime}
and estimates the value of
\phi_{z^{\prime}z}:=\phi_{z^{\prime}}-\phi_{z}
for some fixed
z
, where
\phi_{z^{\prime}}
and
\phi_{z}
area the arguments of the complex phases of
|z\rangle
and
|z^{\prime}\rangle
respectively in
|\$_{s,c}\rangle
.
For all
s,c
, for all
z,z^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
define
{\mathcal{U}}_{z}^{s,c}(z^{\prime})
that takes advice
|\tau_{\mathsf{}}\rangle^{\otimes p(n)}
as follows:

•

For
j=1
to
np(n)^{1/4}
:

–

u_{j}\leftarrow A(s,c,1,z,z^{\prime},0,|\tau_{\mathsf{}}\rangle)

–

v_{j}\leftarrow A(s,c,1,z,z^{\prime},1,|\tau_{\mathsf{}}\rangle)

•

u^{\prime}\leftarrow\sum_{j}u_{j}/np(n)^{1/4}
and
u\leftarrow 2u^{\prime}-1

•

v^{\prime}\leftarrow\sum_{j}v_{j}/np(n)^{1/4}
and
v\leftarrow 2v^{\prime}-1

•

Return
\mathsf{arctan2}(v,u)

The rest of the reduction proceeds as in the phase step of Aaronson’s synthesis, except the oracle calls are replaced with estimates given by
{\mathcal{U}}_{t^{\prime}}^{s,c}(\cdot)
, where
t^{\prime}
is a pivot chosen by measuring an approximation of
|\$^{*}_{s,c}\rangle
.

Superposition queries to
{\mathcal{U}}_{z}^{s,c}(\cdot)
may produce entangled junk so we must later on uncompute to remove the junk.
Let
U_{z}^{s,c}
be the purification of
{\mathcal{U}}_{z}^{s,c}
that acts on input register
\mathsf{Z}
, output register
\mathsf{V}
, and advice register
\mathsf{V}^{\prime}
, i.e.,

U_{z}^{s,c}|z^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}=|z^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}\sum_{v}\sqrt{\Prr[v={\mathcal{U}}_{z}^{s,c}(z^{\prime})]}|v\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\mathsf{junk}_{v}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

where
|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle:=|\tau_{\mathsf{}}\rangle^{\otimes p(n)}|0\rangle
and
|\mathsf{junk}_{v}\rangle
is some normalized state.

Let
P^{\prime}

14
14

14

We may only be able to efficiently implement
P^{\prime}
upto some exponentially small error, however, this small error will not affect our result so we will elide it for the sake of clarity.
be a unitary that maps
|v\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}
to
e^{-iv}|v\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}
. Let
|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle
and
\widetilde{M}
be as defined in Claim
7.2
. Define the algorithm
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}
that takes input
(s,c)
and advice
|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle^{\otimes 2}
and
|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle
as follows:

1.

Compute
\widetilde{M}|s,c\rangle|0^{n}\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle
and measure the second register to get
t^{\prime}
.

2.

Compute
(U_{t^{\prime}}^{s,c})^{\dagger}P^{\prime}U_{t^{\prime}}^{s,c}\left(\widetilde{M}|s,c\rangle_{\mathsf{R}}|0^{n}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\right)|0\rangle_{V}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{V^{\prime}}

3.

Apply
c^{\dagger}
to register
\mathsf{Z}

4.

Return
\mathsf{Z}

Finally, let
{\mathcal{B}}
be the algorithm that takes input
s
, samples
c\leftarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{n}
and outputs
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}(s,c)
using advice
|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle^{\otimes 2}
and
|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle
. Since
|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle=|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{}}\rangle^{\otimes np(n)}
and
|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle=|\tau_{\mathsf{}}\rangle^{\otimes p(n)}|0\rangle
, the algorithm can be executed using advice
|\tau_{\mathsf{synth}}\rangle=|\tau_{\mathsf{}}\rangle^{\otimes(2n+1)p(n)}
.

Analysis.
The reduction estimates
\phi_{z^{\prime}z}
by estimating the probabilities of certain binary outcome measurements. The candidate puzzle
\mathsf{Samp}
is constructed so that any adversary that distributionally inverts the puzzle can be used to approximate the probabilities of the measurements with low error. However, small errors in the probability estimates can still result in large errors in the phase estimate. Essentially,
{\mathcal{U}}_{z}^{s,c}(z^{\prime})
gives a good estimate of
\phi_{z^{\prime}z}
if

(a)

the adversary has low error when
y_{0}=z
and
y_{1}=z^{\prime}
, and

(b)

the weights on
z
and
z^{\prime}
in
|\$_{s,c}\rangle
are not too far from each other.

Applying a unitary 2-design to the state flattens out the weights on the computational basis states, allowing us to argue that the weights on
z^{\prime}
and
z
are close most of the time.

Before we can analyse the above algorithm, we need to define some helpful sets. Recall
k
is a constant such that
n^{k}\geq q(n)
and
p(n)\geq n^{64k}
. For all
s
, we will define
{\mathbb{G}}_{s}
as the set of
c
where the probability that measuring
|\$_{s,c}\rangle
in the computational basis results in a heavy
z
is less than
1/n^{k}
, where a string
z
is heavy if the probability mass of
z
in
|\$_{s,c}\rangle
is greater than
n^{3k}/2^{n}
. Intuitively,
{\mathbb{G}}_{s}
is the set of
c
such that
|\$_{s,c}\rangle
has weight roughly evenly spread over computational basis states
z
, i.e., the total weight on heavy
z
values is small. Formally,
{\mathbb{G}}_{s}:=\left\{c\text{ s.t. }\sum_{z:|\langle{z}|\$_{s,c}\rangle|^{2}\leq n^{3k}/2^{n}}|\langle{z}|\$_{s,c}\rangle|^{2}\leq 1/n^{k}\right\}
. We also define
{\mathbb{S}}
as the set of
s,c
such that the adversary has error less than
1/\sqrt{p}
when
\mathsf{s}=s
and
\mathsf{c}=c
. Formally,
{\mathbb{S}}:=\left\{s,c\text{ s.t. }\Delta_{s,c}\leq 1/\sqrt{p}\right\}
.

SubClaim 7.5
.

For all
s
:

\Prr_{c}[c\in{\mathbb{G}}_{s}]\geq 1-1/n^{k}

Proof.

The proof follows directly from the following theorem, proved in Appendix
B
.

Theorem 7.2

(Flatness of 2-designs)
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a unitary 2-design on
n
qubits. Fix any
n
qubit state
|\psi\rangle
. For any
C\in\mathsf{Supp}({\mathcal{C}})
, let
p_{C}(x):=|\langle x|C|\psi\rangle|^{2}
be the probability that measuring
C|\psi\rangle
in the computational basis results in
x
. Then the following holds for all
k>6
and sufficiently large
n
. Define

{\mathbb{G}}:=\left\{C\in\mathsf{Supp}({\mathcal{C}}):\underset{x:p_{C}(x)\geq\frac{n^{3k}}{2^{n}}}{\sum}p_{C}(x)\leq 1/n^{k}\right\}

Then

\Prr_{C\leftarrow{\mathcal{C}}}[C\in{\mathbb{G}}]\geq 1-1/n^{k}

∎

SubClaim 7.6
.

\Prr_{s,c}[s,c\in{\mathbb{S}}]\geq 1-1/p(n)^{1/2}

Proof.

\Delta_{s,c}
is defined as the error when
\mathsf{s}=s
and
\mathsf{c}=c
. We can write the total error of
{\mathcal{A}}
(which is upper bounded by
1/p(n)
) as the expectation of
\Delta_{s,c}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p(n)}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{s,c}\Prr[\mathsf{s}=s]\cdot\Prr[\mathsf{c}=c]\cdot\Delta_{s,c}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{s,c\notin{\mathbb{S}}}\Prr[\mathsf{s}=s]\cdot\Prr[\mathsf{c}=c]\cdot\Delta_{s,c}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{s,c\notin{\mathbb{S}}}\Prr[\mathsf{s}=s]\cdot\Prr[\mathsf{c}=c]\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{p(n)}},

where the third step follows from the definition of
{\mathbb{S}}
. Therefore,
\Prr_{s,c}[s,c\notin{\mathbb{S}}]\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{p(n)}}
.
∎

Define
{\mathbb{S}}^{\prime}:=\left\{s,c:s,c\in{\mathbb{S}}\wedge c\in{\mathbb{G}}_{s}\right\}
. We first note that with high probability
s,c\in{\mathbb{S}}^{\prime}
.

SubClaim 7.7
.

\Prr_{s,c}[s,c\in{\mathbb{S}}^{\prime}]\geq 1-1/p(n)^{1/2}-1/n^{k}

Proof.

Follows from the definition of
{\mathbb{S}}^{\prime}
and Subclaims
7.5
and
7.6
.
∎

The goal of the remainder of the proof is to show that when
(s,c)\in{\mathbb{S}}^{\prime}
,
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}(s,c)
outputs a state close to
|\$_{s}\rangle\langle\$_{s}|
. Since w.h.p.
(s,c)\in{\mathbb{S}}^{\prime}
, we can ignore case when
(s,c)\notin{\mathbb{S}}^{\prime}
at the cost of some small error probability which is incorporated in the final result. For the rest of the proof, we fix some
(s,c)\in{\mathbb{S}}^{\prime}
, and drop the parameterization on
(s,c)
in the notation.

Define some terms that will be useful for the proof.

•

Interpret
|\$_{s,c}\rangle
as
\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{n}}a_{z}e^{-i\phi_{z}}|z\rangle
where
a_{z}\geq 0
and
\phi_{z}\in[0,2\pi)
, and let
\alpha_{z}:=a_{z}e^{-i\phi_{z}}
and
\phi_{z^{\prime}z}:=\phi_{z^{\prime}}-\phi_{z}

•

|\$^{*}_{s,c}\rangle:=\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{n}}a_{z}|z\rangle
. Intuitively,
|\$_{s,c}^{*}\rangle
represents
|\$_{s,c}\rangle
with the phase information removed, i.e., with real amplitudes.

We now describe how
y_{0}
and
y_{1}
are distributed during the execution of
\mathsf{Samp}
. Define
Y_{0}
as the distribution on
y_{0}
induced by
\mathsf{Samp}
.
Let
R:=\{0,1\}^{n}\setminus\{0^{n}\}
.

SubClaim 7.8
.

For all
y_{0}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

\mathrm{Pr}_{Y_{0}}[y_{0}]=\frac{1}{2}\left(|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{|R|}\right)+\frac{1}{|R|}\right)

Proof.

Recall that
y_{0}
is generated by measuring the
F
register in the computational basis to obtain
x_{0}
, and setting
y_{0}
to be either
x_{0}
or
x_{0}\oplus r
at random (where
r
is uniformly sampled from
R
).

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pr}_{Y_{0}}[y_{0}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|R|}\sum_{r\neq 0}\frac{1}{2}\times\Prr[\text{measuring $F$ register gives $f_{r}(z)=\text{min}(y_{0},y_{0}\oplus r)$}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2|R|}\sum_{r\neq 0}\left|\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes|f_{r}(y_{0})\rangle\langle f_{r}(y_{0})|\right)\left(\sum_{x}\alpha_{x}|x\rangle|f_{r}(x)\rangle\right)\right|^{2}.

Now,
f_{r}(y_{0})=f_{r}(x)
if and only if
x={y_{0}}
or
x={y_{0}}\oplus r
, so only the terms,
\alpha_{y_{0}}|{y_{0}}\rangle|f_{r}({y_{0}})\rangle
and
\alpha_{{y_{0}}\oplus r}|{y_{0}}\oplus r\rangle|f_{r}({y_{0}})\rangle
, are in the support of the projector. Therefore

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pr}_{Y_{0}}[y_{0}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2|R|}\sum_{r\neq 0}\left|\alpha_{y_{0}}|{y_{0}}\rangle+\alpha_{{y_{0}}\oplus r}|{y_{0}}\oplus r\rangle\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2|R|}\sum_{r\neq 0}\left(\left|\alpha_{y_{0}}\right|^{2}+\left|\alpha_{{y_{0}}\oplus r}\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\left|\alpha_{y_{0}}\right|^{2}}{2}+\sum_{y\neq y_{0}}\frac{\left|\alpha_{y}\right|^{2}}{2|R|}.

Since
\sum_{y}\left|\alpha_{y}\right|^{2}=1
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pr}_{Y_{0}}[y_{0}]=\frac{\left|\alpha_{y_{0}}\right|^{2}}{2}+\frac{1-\left|\alpha_{y_{0}}\right|^{2}}{2R},

which after rearranging gives the statement in the claim.
∎

Define
Y^{y_{0}}_{1}
as the distribution on
y_{1}
induced by
\mathsf{Samp}
conditioned on sampling
y_{0}
.

SubClaim 7.9
.

For all
y_{1}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

\mathrm{Pr}_{Y^{y_{0}}_{1}}(y_{1})=\frac{|\alpha_{y_{1}}|^{2}+|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}}{1+|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}(|R|-1)}

Proof.

By the definition of
Y^{y_{0}}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pr}_{Y^{y_{0}}_{1}}[y_{1}]

\displaystyle=\Prr[y_{1}|y_{0}]

\displaystyle=\frac{\Prr[y_{1}\wedge y_{0}]}{\mathrm{Pr}_{Y_{0}}[y_{0}]}

Recall that
y_{0}
and
y_{1}
are a random permutation of
x_{0}
and
x_{0}\oplus r
where
x_{0}
is obtained by measuring the
F
register in the computational basis and
r
is uniformly sampled from
R
. Then,
y_{0}
and
y_{1}
are obtained with probability
\frac{1}{2}
conditioned on
r=y_{0}\oplus y_{1}
and the measurement outcome of
F
is
f_{r}(y_{0})=f_{r}(y_{0}\oplus r)=f_{r}(y_{1})
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pr}_{Y_{1}^{y_{0}}}[y_{1}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{\Prr[\text{measuring $F$ register gives $f_{r}(y_{0})$ }\wedge r={y_{0}}\oplus{y_{1}}]}{\mathrm{Pr}_{Y_{0}}[y_{0}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|R|}\frac{\left|\left(\mathbb{I}\otimes|f_{{y_{0}}\oplus{y_{1}}}({y_{0}})\rangle\langle f_{{y_{0}}\oplus{y_{1}}}({y_{0}})|\right)\left(\sum_{x}\alpha_{x}|x\rangle|f_{{y_{0}}\oplus{y_{1}}}(x)\rangle\right)\right|^{2}}{\mathrm{Pr}_{Y_{0}}[y_{0}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{1}{|R|}\left(|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}+|\alpha_{{y_{1}}}|^{2}\right)}{|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{|R|}\right)+\frac{1}{|R|}}

\displaystyle=\frac{|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}+|\alpha_{{y_{1}}}|^{2}}{1+|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}(|R|-1)},

where the third step follows from Subclaim
7.8
.
∎

Define
\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0}}
as follows:

•

Let
D_{0}
be the distribution of
(\pi,\beta)
when
\pi,\beta
is sampled by
\mathsf{Samp}
conditioned on
\mathsf{Samp}
sampling
y_{0}
and
s=\mathsf{s},c=\mathsf{c},b_{\text{mode}}=1
.

•

Let
D_{1}
be the distribution of
(\pi,A(\pi,|\tau_{\mathsf{}}\rangle))
when
\pi
is sampled by
\mathsf{Samp}
conditioned on
\mathsf{Samp}
sampling
y_{0}
and
s=\mathsf{s},c=\mathsf{c},b_{\text{mode}}=1
.

•

\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0}}:=SD(D_{0},D_{1})

Define
\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0},y_{1}}
as follows:

•

Let
D_{0}
be the distribution of
(\pi,\beta)
when
\pi,\beta
is sampled by
\mathsf{Samp}
conditioned on
\mathsf{Samp}
sampling
y_{0}
and
y_{1}
, while
s=\mathsf{s},c=\mathsf{c},b_{\text{mode}}=1
.

•

Let
D_{1}
be the distribution of
(\pi,A(\pi,|\tau_{\mathsf{}}\rangle))
when
\pi
is sampled by
\mathsf{Samp}
conditioned on on
\mathsf{Samp}
sampling
y_{0}
and
y_{1}
, while
s=\mathsf{s},c=\mathsf{c},b_{\text{mode}}=1
.

•

\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0},y_{1}}:=SD(D_{0},D_{1})

Intuitively,
\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0}}
(and
\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0},y_{1}}
) represent the adversary’s error conditioned on sampling
y_{0}
(and
y_{1}
).

Define the following sets

•

\mathbb{Z}:=\left\{z\text{ s.t. }\Delta^{\prime}_{z}\leq 1/p(n)^{1/4}\right\}

•

\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{z}:=\left\{z^{\prime}\neq z\text{ s.t. }\Delta^{\prime}_{z,z^{\prime}}\leq 1/2p(n)^{1/8}\right\}

•

{\mathbb{L}}:=\left\{z\text{ s.t. }|\alpha_{z}|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{n^{3k}2^{n}}\right\}

•

{\mathbb{U}}:=\left\{z\text{ s.t. }|\alpha_{z}|^{2}\leq\frac{n^{3k}}{2^{n}}\right\}

Intuitively,
\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{z}
are sets of strings on which the adversary’s error is low, while
{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}
is the set of strings whose probability mass in
|\$_{s,c}\rangle
not too far from
1/2^{n}
. The next subclaim shows that

(a)

If
z
is sampled by measuring
|\$^{*}_{s,c}\rangle
, with high probability
z\in\mathbb{Z}\cap{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}

(b)

If
z\in\mathbb{Z}\cap{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}
then most of the probability mass of
|\$_{s,c}\rangle
is on strings
z^{\prime}
such that
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{z}\cap{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}

SubClaim 7.10
.

1.

\sum_{z\notin\mathbb{Z}}|\alpha_{z}|^{2}\leq 3/p(n)^{1/4}

2.

\sum_{z\notin{\mathbb{L}}}|\alpha_{z}|^{2}\leq 1/n^{3k}

3.

\sum_{z\notin{\mathbb{U}}}|\alpha_{z}|^{2}\leq 1/n^{k}

4.

For all
z\in{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}
,
\sum_{z^{\prime}\notin\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{z}}|\alpha_{z^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq 4n^{3k}/p(n)^{1/8}

Proof.

1.

Recall that
\Delta_{s,c}
is the adversary’s error in sampling when
\mathsf{s}=s
and
\mathsf{c}=c
.
\Delta_{s,c}
can therefore be expressed as the expectation over
y_{0}
of
\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0}}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\Delta_{s,c}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{y_{0}}\mathrm{Pr}_{Y_{0}}[y_{0}]\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{y_{0}}\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0}}\left[\frac{|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}}{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{|R|}\right)+\frac{1}{2|R|}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{y_{0}}\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0}}\left[\frac{|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}}{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{|R|}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{y_{0}}\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0}}\frac{|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}}{3},

where the second step uses Subclaim
7.8
. By the definition of
{\mathbb{S}}^{\prime}
,
\Delta_{s,c}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{p(n)}}
, therefore

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{p(n)}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{y_{0}}\frac{\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0}}|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}}{3}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{{y_{0}}\notin\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0}}|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}}{3}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{{y_{0}}\notin\mathbb{Z}}\frac{|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}}{3p(n)^{\frac{1}{4}}},

where the last step follows from the definition of
\mathbb{Z}
. After rearranging and relabeling,

\displaystyle\sum_{z\notin\mathbb{Z}}|\alpha_{z}|^{2}\leq\frac{3}{p(n)^{\frac{1}{4}}}.

2.

By the definition of
{\mathbb{L}}

\displaystyle\sum_{z\notin{\mathbb{L}}}|\alpha_{z}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{z\notin{\mathbb{L}}}\frac{1}{n^{3k}2^{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{z}\frac{1}{n^{3k}2^{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{3k}}.

3.

Recall that
{\mathbb{G}}_{s}=\left\{c\text{ s.t. }\sum_{z:|\langle{z}|\$_{s,c}\rangle|^{2}\leq n^{3k}/2^{n}}|\langle{z}|\$_{s,c}\rangle|^{2}\leq 1/n^{k}\right\}
. We fixed
s,c\in{\mathbb{S}}^{\prime}
, which by definition implies
c\in{\mathbb{G}}_{s}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{k}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{z:|\langle z|\$_{s,c}\rangle|^{2}\leq\frac{n^{3k}}{2^{n}}}|\langle z|\$_{s,c}\rangle|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\notin{\mathbb{U}}}|\langle z|\$_{s,c}\rangle|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\notin{\mathbb{U}}}|\alpha_{z}|^{2}

4.

By the definition of
\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0}}
and
\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0},y_{1}}
,
\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0}}
can be expressed as the expectation over
y_{1}
of
\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0},y_{1}}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{y_{1}}\mathrm{Pr}_{Y_{1}^{y_{0}}}[y_{1}]\cdot\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0},y_{1}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{y_{1}\notin\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{y_{0}}}\mathrm{Pr}_{Y_{1}^{y_{0}}}[y_{1}]\cdot\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0},y_{1}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{y_{1}\notin\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{y_{0}}}\mathrm{Pr}_{Y_{1}^{y_{0}}}[y_{1}]\cdot\frac{1}{2p(n)^{\frac{1}{8}}},

where the last step follows from the definition of
\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{y_{0}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{y_{1}\notin\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{y_{0}}}\mathrm{Pr}_{Y_{1}^{y_{0}}}[y_{1}]

\displaystyle\leq\Delta^{\prime}_{y_{0}}\cdot 2p(n)^{\frac{1}{8}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{p(n)^{\frac{1}{8}}},

where the last step follows from
y_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}
. By Subclaim
7.9
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pr}_{Y_{1}^{y_{0}}}[y_{0}]

\displaystyle=\frac{|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}+|\alpha_{y_{1}}|^{2}}{1+|\alpha_{y_{0}}|^{2}(|R|-1)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{|\alpha_{y_{1}}|^{2}}{1+n^{3k}(|R|-1)2^{-n}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{|\alpha_{y_{1}}|^{2}}{2n^{3k}},

where the second step uses
z\in{\mathbb{U}}
and the last step holds for large enough
n
. Substituting in the previous equation, rearranging, and relabeling gives

\displaystyle\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{z}}|\alpha_{z^{\prime}}|^{2}\geq\frac{4n^{3k}}{p(n)^{\frac{1}{8}}}.

∎

Next we show that
{\mathcal{U}}_{t^{\prime}}(z^{\prime})
gives a good estimate for
\phi_{z^{\prime}t^{\prime}}
when
t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}
and
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\cap{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}
.

SubClaim 7.11
.

For all
z\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}
and for all
z^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{z}
15
15

15

We show that the resulting complex phases are close instead of showing that the angles are close. This is because the estimate of the angle may have a
2\pi
error, but this has no operational meaning when applying the phase.

\Prr\left[\left|e^{-i\cdot{\mathcal{U}}_{z}(z^{\prime})}-e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\right|>\frac{8\sqrt{2}n^{6k}}{p^{1/8}}\right]\leq\mathsf{negl}(n)

Proof.

Fix any
z\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}
and any
z^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{z}
. Recall that for all
x
,
a_{x}=|\alpha_{x}|
and
e^{-i\phi_{x}}=\alpha_{x}/|\alpha_{x}|
. Also recall that
\phi_{z^{\prime}z}=\phi_{z^{\prime}}-\phi_{z}
. Consider the state
|\psi_{\text{post}}\rangle
conditioned on obtaining
y_{0}=z
and
y_{1}=z^{\prime}
during the execution of
\mathsf{Samp}
.

|\psi_{\text{post}}\rangle=\frac{\alpha_{z}|z\rangle+\alpha_{z^{\prime}}|z^{\prime}\rangle}{\sqrt{|\alpha_{z}|^{2}+|\alpha_{z^{\prime}}|^{2}}}=e^{-i\phi_{z}}\cdot\frac{a_{z}|z\rangle+a_{z^{\prime}}e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}|z^{\prime}\rangle}{\sqrt{|\alpha_{z}|^{2}+|\alpha_{z^{\prime}}|^{2}}}

Let
\theta
be the unique angle in
[0,\pi]
such that
\cos\frac{\theta}{2}=\frac{a_{z}}{\sqrt{a_{z}^{2}+a_{z^{\prime}}^{2}}}
and
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}=\frac{a_{z^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{a_{z}^{2}+a_{z^{\prime}}^{2}}}
. Let
|\psi^{\prime}\rangle:=e^{i\phi_{z}}|\psi_{\text{post}}\rangle
. Then

|\psi^{\prime}\rangle=\cos\left(\theta/2\right)|z\rangle+e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\sin\left(\theta/2\right)|z^{\prime}\rangle

Let
A^{\prime}
be the probability that applying
V_{z,z^{\prime},0}
to
|\psi_{\text{post}}\rangle
and measuring results in output
z
. We first obtain an expression for
A^{\prime}
in terms of
\theta
and
\phi_{z^{\prime}z}
by ignoring global phase and expanding

\displaystyle A^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left|\langle z|V_{z,z^{\prime},0}|\psi_{\text{post}}\rangle\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\langle z|V_{z,z^{\prime},0}|\psi^{\prime}\rangle\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\langle z|\left(\frac{\cos\left(\theta/2\right)+e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\sin\left(\theta/2\right)}{\sqrt{2}}|z\rangle+\frac{\cos\left(\theta/2\right)-e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\sin\left(\theta/2\right)}{\sqrt{2}}|z^{\prime}\rangle\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\cos\left(\theta/2\right)+e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\sin\left(\theta/2\right)}{\sqrt{2}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\cos\left(\theta/2\right)+e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\sin\left(\theta/2\right)}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot\frac{\cos\left(\theta/2\right)+e^{i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\sin\left(\theta/2\right)}{\sqrt{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\cos^{2}\left(\theta/2\right)+\sin^{2}\left(\theta/2\right)+2\cos\left(\theta/2\right)\sin\left(\theta/2\right)\left(e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}+e^{i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\right)}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+\sin\theta\left(e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}+e^{i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\right)}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+\sin\theta\cos\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}{2}

Similarly, let
B^{\prime}
be the probability that applying
V_{z,z^{\prime},1}
to
|\psi_{\text{post}}\rangle
and measuring results in output
z
. Then

\displaystyle B^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left|\langle z|V_{z,z^{\prime},1}|\psi_{\text{post}}\rangle\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\langle z|V_{z,z^{\prime},1}|\psi^{\prime}\rangle\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\langle z|\left(\frac{\cos\left(\theta/2\right)+ie^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\sin\left(\theta/2\right)}{\sqrt{2}}|z\rangle+\frac{\cos\left(\theta/2\right)-ie^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\sin\left(\theta/2\right)}{\sqrt{2}}|z^{\prime}\rangle\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\cos\left(\theta/2\right)+ie^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\sin\left(\theta/2\right)}{\sqrt{2}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\cos\left(\theta/2\right)+ie^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\sin\left(\theta/2\right)}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot\frac{\cos\left(\theta/2\right)-ie^{i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\sin\left(\theta/2\right)}{\sqrt{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\cos^{2}\left(\theta/2\right)+\sin^{2}\left(\theta/2\right)+2i\cos\left(\theta/2\right)\sin\left(\theta/2\right)\left(e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}-e^{i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\right)}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+\sin\theta\left(ie^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}-ie^{i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\right)}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+\sin\theta\sin\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}{2}

Let
A:=2A^{\prime}-1=\sin\theta\cos\phi_{z^{\prime}z}
and
B:=2B^{\prime}-1=\sin\theta\sin\phi_{z^{\prime}z}
. We note that since
\theta\in[0,\pi)
,
\sin\theta\geq 0
and therefore
\mathsf{arctan2}(B,A)=\phi_{z^{\prime}z}
.

Let
\widetilde{A}^{\prime}:=\mathrm{Pr}[1={\mathcal{A}}(s,c,1,z,z^{\prime},0)]
and let
\widetilde{B}^{\prime}:=\mathrm{Pr}[1={\mathcal{A}}(s,c,1,z,z^{\prime},1)]
. Since
z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{z}

\displaystyle 1/2p(n)^{1/8}

\displaystyle\geq\Delta^{\prime}_{z,z^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\left|\widetilde{A}^{\prime}-A^{\prime}\right|+\left|\widetilde{B}^{\prime}-B^{\prime}\right|\right)

where the second step follows directly from the definition of
\Delta^{\prime}_{z,z^{\prime}}
. As a result

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{A}^{\prime}-A^{\prime}\right|\leq 1/p(n)^{1/8}

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{B}^{\prime}-B^{\prime}\right|\leq 1/p(n)^{1/8}

Let
\widetilde{A}:=2\widetilde{A}^{\prime}-1
and
\widetilde{B}:=2\widetilde{B}^{\prime}-1
. Therefore

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{A}-A\right|\leq 2/p(n)^{1/8}

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{B}-B\right|\leq 2/p(n)^{1/8}

i.e.
\widetilde{A}
is close to
A
and
\widetilde{B}
is close to
B
.Consider the case when
{\mathcal{U}}_{z}
is run on
z^{\prime}
and internally samples
u
and
v
. By setting
\delta=\sqrt{n}
in the additive Chernoff bound (Theorem
4.1
), we see that
u
and
v
that are computed by
{\mathcal{U}}_{z}
are good approximations of
\widetilde{A}
and
\widetilde{B}
, and thus of
A
and
B
. Formally,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pr}\left[\left|u-\widetilde{A}\right|\geq\frac{2}{p(n)^{1/8}}\right]\leq 2e^{-2n}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pr}\left[\left|v-\widetilde{B}\right|\geq\frac{2}{p(n)^{1/8}}\right]\leq 2e^{-2n}

Using the fact that
\widetilde{A}
and
\widetilde{B}
are close to
A
and
B
as shown above, we can bound the Euclidean distance between
(u,v)
and
(A,B)

\displaystyle 1-4e^{-2n}

\displaystyle\leq\Prr\left[\left|v-\widetilde{B}\right|\leq\frac{2}{p(n)^{1/8}}\text{ and }\left|u-\widetilde{A}\right|\leq\frac{2}{p(n)^{1/8}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Prr\left[\left|v-B\right|\leq\frac{4}{p(n)^{1/8}}\text{ and }\left|u-A\right|\leq\frac{4}{p(n)^{1/8}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Prr\left[\left(v-B\right)^{2}+\left(u-A\right)^{2}\leq\left(\frac{4\sqrt{2}}{p(n)^{1/8}}\right)^{2}\right]

(12)

We will use the following theorem which we prove in Appendix
C

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
(x,y),(x^{*},y^{*})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
such that
\exists\gamma>0,\gamma^{\prime}>0
s.t.

•

x^{2}+y^{2}\geq\gamma^{2}

•

(x-x^{*})^{2}+(y-y^{*})^{2}\leq(\gamma^{\prime})^{2}

•

\gamma^{\prime}<\gamma

Then
|e^{-i\cdot\mathsf{arctan2}(y,x)}-e^{-i\cdot\mathsf{arctan2}(y^{*},x^{*})}|\leq 2\gamma^{\prime}/\gamma

Using the fact that both
z
and
z^{\prime}
belong to
{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}
, we can show that
(A,B)
is atleast
1/n^{6k}
far from the origin.

\displaystyle A^{2}+B^{2}

\displaystyle=\sin^{2}\theta\left(\cos^{2}\phi_{z^{\prime}z}+\sin^{2}\phi_{z^{\prime}z}\right)

\displaystyle=\sin^{2}\theta

\displaystyle=\left(2\sin(\theta/2)\cos(\theta/2)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{2a_{z}a_{z^{\prime}}}{a^{2}_{z}+a^{2}_{z^{\prime}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{2}{a_{z}/a_{z^{\prime}}+a_{z^{\prime}}/a_{z}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{2\cdot\frac{1}{n^{3k}2^{n}}}{2n^{3k}/2^{n}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1/n^{6k}\right)^{2}

where the fourth step follows from the definition of
\theta
and the fifth step follows from bounds on
a_{z}
and
a_{z^{\prime}}
implied by
z,z^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}
.
Recall that
p(n)>n^{64k}
and
k>6
. Therefore, for large enough
n
,
p(n)>4\sqrt{2/n^{6k}}
. Equation (
12
) thus implies that we can apply Theorem
7.3
with probability atleast
1-4e^{-2n}
when we set
(x,y)=(A,B),(x^{*},y^{*})=(u,v),\gamma=1/n^{6k}
and
\gamma^{\prime}=4\sqrt{2}/p(n)^{1/8}
. Formally,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pr}\left[\left|e^{-i\cdot\mathsf{arctan2}(v,u)}-e^{-i\cdot\mathsf{arctan2}(B,A)}\right|\leq\left(\frac{8\sqrt{2}n^{6k}}{p(n)^{1/8}}\right)\right]\geq 1-4e^{-2n}

\displaystyle\implies\Prr\left[\left|e^{-i\cdot{\mathcal{U}}_{z}(z^{\prime})}-e^{-i\cdot\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\right|>\frac{8\sqrt{2}n^{6k}}{p(n)^{1/8}}\right]\leq\mathsf{negl}(n)

which concludes the proof of the subclaim.
∎

Next we show that if
z\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}
, then the oracle responses in the phase step of Aaronson’s synthesis can be answered by the estimator’s outputs.

SubClaim 7.12
.

For all
z\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}
, for sufficiently large
n

\left||\$_{s,c}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}-e^{-i\phi^{z,c}_{z}}(U_{z})^{\dagger}P^{\prime}U_{z}|\$^{*}_{s,c}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}\right|\leq\frac{8\sqrt{2}n^{6k}}{p(n)^{1/8}}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{n^{k}}}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{n^{3k}}}+\frac{4\sqrt{n^{3k}}}{p(n)^{1/16}}

Proof.

Applying a global phase to the difference does not alter the magnitude, so we multiply by
e^{-i\phi_{z}}

\displaystyle\zeta:=

\displaystyle\left||\$\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}-e^{-i\phi_{z}}U_{z}^{\dagger}P^{\prime}U_{z}|\$^{*}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|e^{i\phi_{z}}U_{z}|\$\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}-P^{\prime}U_{z}|\$^{*}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}\right|

(13)

Next, we use the definition of
U_{z}
and
P^{\prime}
to expand each term. Expanding
e^{i\phi_{z}}U_{z}|\$\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle e^{i\phi_{z}}U_{z}|\$\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z^{\prime}}\alpha_{z^{\prime}}e^{i\phi_{z}}|z^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}\sum_{v}\sqrt{\Prr[v={\mathcal{U}}_{z}(z^{\prime})]}|v\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\mathsf{junk}_{v}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z^{\prime}}a_{z^{\prime}}e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}|z^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}\sum_{v}\sqrt{\Prr[v={\mathcal{U}}_{z}(z^{\prime})]}|v\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\mathsf{junk}_{v}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

(14)

Expanding
P^{\prime}U_{z}|\$^{*}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle P^{\prime}U_{z}|\$^{*}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|0\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z^{\prime}}a_{z^{\prime}}|z^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}\sum_{v}e^{-iv}\sqrt{\Prr[v={\mathcal{U}}_{z}(z^{\prime})]}|v\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\mathsf{junk}_{v}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}

(15)

Define
{\mathbb{A}}:={\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}
and
{\mathbb{B}}:={\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{z}
. Plugging (
14
) and (
15
) into (
13
) and squaring gives

\displaystyle\zeta^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{z^{\prime}}a_{z^{\prime}}|z^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}\sum_{v}\left(e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}-e^{-iv}\right)\sqrt{\Prr[v={\mathcal{U}}_{z}(z^{\prime})]}|v\rangle_{\mathsf{V}}|\mathsf{junk}_{v}\rangle_{\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z^{\prime}}a^{2}_{z^{\prime}}\sum_{v}\Prr[v={\mathcal{U}}_{z}(z^{\prime})]\left|e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}-e^{-iv}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{z^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{B}}}a^{2}_{z^{\prime}}\sum_{v}\Prr[v={\mathcal{U}}_{z}(z^{\prime})]\left|e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}-e^{-iv}\right|^{2}+\sum_{z^{\prime}\notin{\mathbb{B}}}4a^{2}_{z^{\prime}}

(16)

Let
\delta:=\frac{8\sqrt{2}n^{6k}}{p(n)^{1/8}}
. For all
z^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{B}}
, let
{\mathbb{V}}_{z^{\prime}}:=\left\{v\text{ s.t. }\left|e^{-iv}-e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}\right|\leq\delta\right\}
. Then SubClaim
7.11
shows that the probability that when
z^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{B}}
, the probability that
{\mathcal{U}}_{z}(z^{\prime})
outputs
v\notin{\mathbb{V}}_{z^{\prime}}
is negligible. Therefore

\displaystyle\sum_{v}\Prr[v={\mathcal{U}}_{z}(z^{\prime})]\left|e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}-e^{-iv}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in{\mathbb{V}}_{z^{\prime}}}\Prr[v={\mathcal{U}}_{z}(z^{\prime})]\left|e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}-e^{-iv}\right|^{2}+\sum_{v\notin{\mathbb{V}}_{z^{\prime}}}4\Prr[v={\mathcal{U}}_{z}(z^{\prime})]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in{\mathbb{V}}_{z^{\prime}}}\Prr[v={\mathcal{U}}_{z}(z^{\prime})]\left|e^{-i\phi_{z^{\prime}z}}-e^{-iv}\right|^{2}+\mathsf{negl}(n)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in{\mathbb{V}}_{z^{\prime}}}\Prr[v={\mathcal{U}}_{z}(z^{\prime})]\delta^{2}+\mathsf{negl}(n)

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{2}+\mathsf{negl}(n)

Plugging back in (
16
) gives

\displaystyle\zeta^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{z^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{B}}}a^{2}_{z^{\prime}}\delta^{2}+\sum_{z^{\prime}\notin{\mathbb{B}}}4a^{2}_{z^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{2}+\sum_{z^{\prime}\notin{\mathbb{B}}}4a^{2}_{z^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{2}+4/n^{k}+4/n^{3k}+16n^{3k}/p^{1/8}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{128n^{12k}}{p(n)^{1/4}}+4/n^{k}+4/n^{3k}+\frac{16n^{3k}}{p(n)^{1/8}}

(17)

where the third step follows from parts 2, 3, and 4 of SubClaim
7.10
and the last step substitutes the value of
\delta
. The final expression follows from taking the square root of both sides and noting that the square root function is subadditive.
∎

We can now begin analyzing the algorithm
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}(s,c)
, dropping the advice state from the input for notational convenience. For all
t
, define
|\sigma_{t}\rangle
as follows:

|\sigma_{t}\rangle:=(U_{t})^{\dagger}P^{\prime}U_{t}\left(\widetilde{M}_{\text{amp}}|s,c\rangle_{\mathsf{R}}|0^{n}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\right)|0\rangle_{V}|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle_{V^{\prime}}

and define
\rho_{t}
as the state on the
\mathsf{Z}
register of
|\sigma_{t}\rangle\langle\sigma_{t}|
after tracing out the remaining registers, i.e.

\rho_{t}=\mathsf{Tr}_{\mathsf{R}\mathsf{A}\mathsf{V}\mathsf{V}^{\prime}}\left(|\sigma_{t}\rangle\langle\sigma_{t}|\right)

Note that the output of
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}(s,c)
conditioned sampling
t
is
c^{\dagger}\rho_{t}c
. Next we show that
c^{\dagger}\rho_{t}c
is close to
|\$_{s}\rangle\langle\$_{s}|
when
t\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}
. By
(s,c)\in{\mathbb{S}}^{\prime}
and the definition of
{\mathbb{S}}
,
\Delta_{s,c}\leq 1/\sqrt{p(n)}
. Let
\delta_{\text{amp}}:=(\sqrt{3n^{3}}+2\sqrt{n})/p(n)^{1/4}
(i.e. the error term in Claim
7.2
after substituting
\Delta_{s,c}\leq 1/\sqrt{p(n)}
) and let
\delta_{\text{phase}}:=\frac{8\sqrt{2}n^{6k}}{p(n)^{1/8}}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{n^{k}}}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{n^{3k}}}+\frac{4\sqrt{n^{3k}}}{p(n)^{1/16}}
(i.e. the error term in SubClaim
7.12
).

SubClaim 7.13
.

If
t\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}
then

\langle\$_{s}|c^{\dagger}\cdot\rho_{t}\cdot c|\$_{s}\rangle\geq 1-\delta_{\text{amp}}-\delta_{\text{phase}}

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle|\psi_{1}\rangle:=|s,c\rangle|0^{n}\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle

\displaystyle|\psi_{2}\rangle:=|s,c\rangle|\$^{*}_{s,c}\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle

\displaystyle|\psi_{3}\rangle:=e^{i\phi_{t}}|s,c\rangle|\$_{s,c}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{1}}\rangle

\displaystyle M_{1}:=\widetilde{M}_{\text{amp}}

\displaystyle M_{2}:=(U^{s,c}_{t})^{\dagger}P^{\prime}U^{s,c}_{t}.

By
(s,c)\in{\mathbb{S}}^{\prime}
and the definition of
{\mathbb{S}}
,
\Delta_{s,c}\leq 1/\sqrt{p(n)}
. Therefore, Claim
7.2
can be restated as

\displaystyle|M_{1}|\psi_{1}\rangle-|\psi_{2}\rangle|\leq\delta_{\text{amp}}.

Similarly, SubClaim
7.12
can be restated as

\displaystyle|M_{2}|\psi_{2}\rangle-|\psi_{3}\rangle|\leq\delta_{\text{phase}}.

By the triangle inequality and subsituting the last two equations in the last step,

\displaystyle|M_{2}M_{1}|\psi_{1}\rangle-|\psi_{3}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq|M_{2}M_{1}|\psi_{1}\rangle-M_{2}|\psi_{2}\rangle|+|M_{2}|\psi_{2}\rangle-|\psi_{3}\rangle|

\displaystyle=|M_{1}|\psi_{1}\rangle-|\psi_{2}\rangle|+|M_{2}|\psi_{2}\rangle-|\psi_{3}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{\text{amp}}+\delta_{\text{phase}}.

By Theorem
4.2
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}(M_{2}M_{1}|\psi_{1}\rangle\langle\psi_{1}|M_{1}^{\dagger}M_{2}^{\dagger},|\psi_{3}\rangle\langle\psi_{3}|)\leq\delta_{\text{amp}}+\delta_{\text{phase}}.

Noting that
|\sigma_{t}\rangle
is defined as
M_{2}M_{1}|\psi_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}(|\sigma_{t}\rangle\langle\sigma_{t}|,|\psi_{3}\rangle\langle\psi_{3}|)\leq\delta_{\text{amp}}+\delta_{\text{phase}},

We can trace out all but the
\mathsf{Z}
register from both states without increasing the trace distance. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}(\rho_{t},|\$_{s,c}\rangle\langle\$_{s,c}|)\leq\delta_{\text{amp}}+\delta_{\text{phase}}.

Consider the projector
|\$_{s,c}\rangle\langle\$_{s,c}|
. The projection succeeds on the state
|\$_{s,c}\rangle\langle\$_{s,c}|
with probability 1, so it must succeed on
\rho_{t}
with probability atleast
1-\mathsf{TD}(\rho_{t},|\$_{s,c}\rangle\langle\$_{s,c}|)\geq 1-\delta_{\text{amp}}-\delta_{\text{phase}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\langle\$_{s}|c^{\dagger}\rho_{t}c|\$_{s}\rangle=\langle\$_{s,c}|\rho_{t}|\$_{s,c}\rangle\geq 1-\delta_{\text{amp}}-\delta_{\text{phase}}.

∎

Next we show that with high probability,
t\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}
. Let
\delta_{\text{samp}}:=3/p(n)^{1/4}-1/n^{k}-1/n^{3k}
(i.e. the sum of error terms from parts 1, 2, and 3 of SubClaim
7.10
).

SubClaim 7.14
.

Let
t
be the outcome when measuring the
\mathsf{Z}
register of
\widetilde{M}_{\text{amp}}|s,c\rangle_{\mathsf{R}}|0^{n}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}
in the computational basis. Then

\mathrm{Pr}[t\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}]\geq 1-\delta_{\text{samp}}-\delta_{\text{amp}}

Proof.

Consider the probability of obtaining a string
t^{\prime}
upon measuring the second register of
|s,c\rangle|\$^{*}_{s,c}\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle
.

\mathrm{Pr}[t^{\prime}]=\left|\langle t^{\prime}|\sum_{z}a_{z}|z\rangle\right|^{2}=\left(a_{z}\right)^{2}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pr}[t^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}]=

\displaystyle\sum_{z\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}}\left(a_{z}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-\sum_{z\notin{\mathbb{L}}}\left(a_{z}\right)^{2}-\sum_{z\notin{\mathbb{U}}}\left(a_{z}\right)^{2}-\sum_{z\notin\mathbb{Z}}\left(a_{z}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-3/p(n)^{1/4}-1/n^{k}-1/n^{3k}=1-\delta_{\text{samp}}

where the last step follows from parts 1, 2, and 3 of SubClaim
7.10
, noting that
a_{z}=|\alpha_{z}|
. By Claim
7.2
and Theorem
4.2
we know that
\widetilde{M}_{\text{amp}}|s,c\rangle|0\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle
and
|s,c\rangle|\$^{*}_{s,c}\rangle|\tau_{\mathsf{amp}}\rangle
are atmost
\delta_{\text{amp}}
apart in trace distance. Therefore, the output distributions upon measuring each state can be atmost
\delta_{\text{amp}}
far. Therefore,

\mathrm{Pr}[t\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}]\geq 1-\delta_{\text{samp}}-\delta_{\text{amp}}

∎

Putting together SubClaim
7.14

and SubClaim
7.13
shows that the expected overlap of
|\$_{s}\rangle\langle\$_{s}|
and
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}(s,c)
is high.

SubClaim 7.15
.

\underset{{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\langle\$_{s}|{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}(s,c)|\$_{s}\rangle\right]\geq 1-\delta_{\text{samp}}-2\delta_{\text{amp}}-\delta_{\text{phase}}

Proof.

SubClaim
7.13
shows that if
t
sampled by
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}
is in
{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}
then the overlap is atleast
1-\delta_{\text{amp}}-\delta_{\text{phase}}
, while SubClaim
7.14
shows that
t\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}
occurs with probability atleast
1-\delta_{\text{samp}}-\delta_{\text{amp}}
. Putting these together

\displaystyle\underset{{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\langle\$_{s}|{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}(s,c)|\$_{s}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{Pr}[t\in{\mathbb{L}}\cap{\mathbb{U}}\cap\mathbb{Z}]\cdot(1-\delta_{\text{amp}}-\delta_{\text{phase}})

\displaystyle\geq(1-\delta_{\text{samp}}-\delta_{\text{amp}})\cdot(1-\delta_{\text{amp}}-\delta_{\text{phase}})

\displaystyle\geq 1-\delta_{\text{samp}}-2\delta_{\text{amp}}-\delta_{\text{phase}}

where the first step follows from SubClaim
7.13
and the second step follows from SubClaim
7.14
.
∎

Finally, we show that
{\mathcal{B}}
achieves the claimed bound. We note that SubClaim
7.15
applies for arbitrary fixed
(s,c)\in{\mathbb{S}}^{\prime}
and SubClaim
7.7
shows that for
s
sampled by
{\mathcal{G}}
and
c\leftarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{n}
the probability that
(s,c)\in{\mathbb{S}}
is atleast
1-1/p(n)^{1/2}-1/n^{k}
. Putting them together

\displaystyle\underset{s,|\$_{s}\rangle\leftarrow{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})}{{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\langle\$_{s}|{\mathcal{B}}(s,|\tau_{\mathsf{synth}}\rangle)|\$_{s}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{Pr}[(s,c)\in{\mathbb{S}}^{\prime}]\cdot(1-\delta_{\text{samp}}-2\delta_{\text{amp}}-\delta_{\text{phase}})

\displaystyle\geq(1-1/p(n)^{1/2}-1/n^{k})\cdot(1-\delta_{\text{samp}}-2\delta_{\text{amp}}-\delta_{\text{phase}})

\displaystyle\geq 1-1/p(n)^{1/2}-1/n^{k}-\delta_{\text{samp}}-2\delta_{\text{amp}}-\delta_{\text{phase}}

All that remains it to simplify the expression. Substituting the definitions of
\delta_{\text{samp}}
,
\delta_{\text{amp}}
, and
\delta_{\text{phase}}
, noting that
k>6
and
p(n)>n^{64k}
and simplifying for large enough
n

\displaystyle 1-1/p(n)^{1/2}-1/n^{k}-\delta_{\text{samp}}-2\delta_{\text{amp}}-\delta_{\text{phase}}\geq 1-3/\sqrt{n^{k}}

Finally, since
n^{k}\geq q(n)^{3}

\displaystyle\underset{s,|\$_{s}\rangle\leftarrow{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})}{{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\langle\$_{s}|{\mathcal{B}}(s,|\tau_{\mathsf{synth}}\rangle)|\$_{s}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-3/q(n)^{3/2}\geq 1-1/q(n)

which concludes the proof of the Claim.
∎

Claim
7.3
shows the existence of an algorithm that contradicts the security of
{\mathcal{G}}
which concludes the proof of the theorem.∎

Next, we prove that the existence of distributional one-way puzzles implies the existance of (standard) state puzzles.

Theorem 7.4
.

If one-way puzzles (Definition
4.2
) exist then state puzzles (Definition
7.1
) exist.

Since Theorem
4.3
shows that distributional one-way puzzles can be amplified to obtain (strong) one-way puzzles, the following is a corollary of Theorem
7.4
.

Corollary 7.2
.

If
1/q(n)
-distributional one-way puzzles (Definition
4.3
) exist for some non-zero polynomial
q(\cdot)
then state puzzles (Definition
7.1
) exist.

of Theorem
7.4
.

Let
(\mathsf{Samp}(1^{n}),\mathsf{Ver})
be a one-way puzzle that samples
n
bit puzzles and
n
bit keys. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\mathsf{Samp}(1^{n})
is the algorithm that first applies a unitary
U_{n}
to
|0\rangle
to obtain

U_{n}|0\rangle=\sum_{s,k}\sqrt{p_{s,k}}|\mu_{s,k}\rangle|s\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}|k\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}

where
\{\mu_{s,k}\}_{s,k}
are a set of normalised states,
s
and
k
are an
n
-bit puzzle and and
n
-bit key respectively output by
\mathsf{Samp}(1^{n})
with probability
p_{s,k}
. This is followed by a classical basis measurement of registers
\mathsf{S}
and
\mathsf{K}
to obtain puzzle
s
and key
k
.

Let
{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})
be the following algorithm

1.

Apply
U_{n}
to
|0\rangle
to obtain
\sum_{s,k}\sqrt{p_{s,k}}|\mu_{s,k}\rangle|s\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}|k\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}

2.

Measure
\mathsf{S}
in the classical basis to obtain string
s
and residual state
|\psi_{s}\rangle
.

3.

Return
(s,|\psi_{s}\rangle)
.

We will prove that
{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})
is a state puzzle, which sufficies to prove the theorem. Suppose for the sake of contradiction that
{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})
is not a state puzzle. There there exists a polynomial
p(n)
. QPT
{\mathcal{A}}=\{{\mathcal{A}}_{n}\}_{{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}}
, and an advice ensemble
|\tau\rangle=\{|\tau_{n}\rangle\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that for all large enough
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

\underset{(s,|\psi_{s}\rangle)\leftarrow{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})}{{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\langle\psi_{s}|{\mathcal{A}}(|\tau\rangle,s)|\psi_{s}\rangle\right]\geq\frac{1}{p(n)}

Fix any such adversary
{\mathcal{A}}
, any such advice ensemble
|\tau\rangle
, and any such large enough
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. We will drop the advice from the notation since it is always implicitly provided to the adversary.

Define the algorithm
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
that takes input
s
, computes
{\mathcal{A}}(s)
, and outputs the outcome of measuring the
\mathsf{K}
register of
{\mathcal{A}}(s)
in the computational basis. We will show that
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
contradicts the security of the one-way puzzle
(\mathsf{Samp}(1^{n}),\mathsf{Ver})
.

Let
p_{s}
be the probability that
\mathsf{Samp}(1^{n})
samples puzzle
s
. Note that this is identical to the probability that
{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})
samples
s
since in both cases
s
is generated the same way. We first show that the expected trace distance between
|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|
and
{\mathcal{A}}(s)
is at most
\sqrt{1-1/p(n)}
.

Claim 7.4
.

\underset{(s,|\psi_{s}\rangle)\leftarrow{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})}{{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathsf{TD}\Big(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|,{\mathcal{A}}(s)\Big)\right]\leq\sqrt{1-1/p(n)}

Proof.

For any
s
, the fidelity
F({\mathcal{A}}(s),|\psi_{s}\rangle)
of
{\mathcal{A}}(s)
and
|\psi_{s}\rangle
is
\sqrt{\langle\psi_{s}|{\mathcal{A}}(s)|\psi_{s}\rangle}
. Therefore, by Uhlmann’s Theorem, for any
s

\displaystyle\mathsf{TD}\Big(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|,{\mathcal{A}}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{1-F({\mathcal{A}}(s),|\psi_{s}\rangle)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{1-\langle\psi_{s}|{\mathcal{A}}(s)|\psi_{s}\rangle}

Expressing
\underset{(s,|\psi_{s}\rangle)\leftarrow{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})}{{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathsf{TD}\Big(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|,{\mathcal{A}}(s)\Big)\right]
as a sum over
s
and applying the above

\displaystyle\underset{(s,|\psi_{s}\rangle)\leftarrow{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})}{{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathsf{TD}\Big(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|,{\mathcal{A}}(s)\Big)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{s}p_{s}\cdot\mathsf{TD}\Big(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|,{\mathcal{A}}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s}p_{s}\cdot\sqrt{1-\langle\psi_{s}|{\mathcal{A}}(s)|\psi_{s}\rangle}

Applying Jensen’s inequality

\displaystyle\underset{(s,|\psi_{s}\rangle)\leftarrow{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})}{{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathsf{TD}\Big(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|,{\mathcal{A}}(s)\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{s}p_{s}\cdot\left(1-\langle\psi_{s}|{\mathcal{A}}(s)|\psi_{s}\rangle\right)}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{1-\sum_{s}p_{s}\cdot\langle\psi_{s}|{\mathcal{A}}(s)|\psi_{s}\rangle}

Now, we can rewrite the fact that
\underset{(s,|\psi_{s}\rangle)\leftarrow{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})}{{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\langle\psi_{s}|{\mathcal{A}}(s)|\psi_{s}\rangle\right]\geq\frac{1}{p(n)}
as a sum over
s
.

\displaystyle\sum_{s}p_{s}\cdot\langle\psi_{s}|{\mathcal{A}}(s)|\psi_{s}\rangle\geq 1/p(n)

which when plugged into the previous inequality implies

\underset{(s,|\psi_{s}\rangle)\leftarrow{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})}{{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathsf{TD}\Big(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|,{\mathcal{A}}(s)\Big)\right]\leq\sqrt{1-1/p(n)}

∎

Claim 7.5
.

\Prr_{(s,k)\leftarrow\mathsf{Samp}(1^{n})}\left[\mathsf{Ver}(s,{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}(s))=1\right]\geq 1/3p(n)

Proof.

By the correctness of the one-way puzzle

\Prr_{(s,k)\leftarrow\mathsf{Samp}(1^{n})}[\mathsf{Ver}(s,k)=1]\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(n)

Let
M
be an algorithm that takes input
|\psi\rangle
and returns the result
k
of measuring the
\mathsf{K}
register in the computational basis. The distribution over
s
and
k
obtained by sampling
(s,|\psi_{s}\rangle)
from
{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})
and sampling
k
from
M(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|)
is therefore identical to the distribution obtained by sampling
(s,k)
from
\mathsf{Samp}(1^{n})
.
We can express the probability that
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}(s)
successfully outputs a key that passes verification as a sum over
s
. Therefore

\Prr_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s,|\psi_{s}\rangle)\leftarrow{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})\\
k\leftarrow M(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|)\end{subarray}}[\mathsf{Ver}(s,k)=1]\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(n)

which may be rewritten as

\Prr_{(s,|\psi_{s}\rangle)\leftarrow{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})}[\mathsf{Ver}(s,M(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|))=1]\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(n)

and expressed as a sum over
s
as follows.

\sum_{s}p_{s}\cdot\Prr[\mathsf{Ver}(s,M(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|))=1]\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(n)

For any
s
and any state
\rho
,

\Big|\mathrm{Pr}\Big[\mathsf{Ver}(s,M(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|))=1\Big]-\mathrm{Pr}\Big[\mathsf{Ver}(s,M(\rho))=1\Big]\Big|\leq\mathsf{TD}(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|,\rho)

which means that we can replace
|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|
with
{\mathcal{A}}(s)
at the cost of an error of
\mathsf{TD}(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|,{\mathcal{A}}(s))
, i.e.

\displaystyle 1-\mathsf{negl}(n)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s}p_{s}\cdot\Big(\Prr\Big[\mathsf{Ver}\Big(s,M({\mathcal{A}}(s))\Big)=1\Big]+\mathsf{TD}\Big(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|,{\mathcal{A}}(s)\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s}p_{s}\cdot\Big(\Prr\Big[\mathsf{Ver}\Big(s,M({\mathcal{A}}(s))\Big)=1\Big]\Big)+\underset{s,|\psi_{s}\rangle\leftarrow{\mathcal{G}}(1^{n})}{{\mathbb{E}}}\left[\mathsf{TD}\Big(|\psi_{s}\rangle\langle\psi_{s}|,{\mathcal{A}}(s)\Big)\right]

The first term is exactly
\Prr_{s,k\leftarrow\mathsf{Samp}(1^{n})}\left[\mathsf{Ver}(s,{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}(s))=1\right]
and the second term is shown in Claim
7.4
upper bounded by
\sqrt{1-p(n)}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\Prr_{s,k\leftarrow\mathsf{Samp}(1^{n})}\left[\mathsf{Ver}(s,{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}(s))=1\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(n)-\sqrt{1-p(n)}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(n)-(1-p(n)/2)

\displaystyle\geq p(n)/2-\mathsf{negl}(n)

\displaystyle\geq p(n)/3

where the last step holds for large enough
n
.
∎

This shows that
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
contradicts the security of the one-way puzzle, concluding the proof of the theorem.
∎

We also have the following straightforward corollary, which follows from the implication of state puzzles from quantum money mini-schemes (and other unclonable primitives).

Corollary 7.3
.

Quantum money mini-schemes imply one-way puzzles and quantum bit commitments.

Appendix A
Instantiating Uniform Approximation Hardness (Definition
5.1
)

In this section we will show how to instantiate uniform approximation hardness with well studied conjectures from the sampling-based quantum advantage literature. Specifically, we will import some conjectures from the literature on BosonSampling, Random Circuit Sampling, IQP and DQC1 sampling; and will discuss why they imply Definition
5.1
.

A.1
Random Circuit Sampling

The exposition in this section is primarily taken from
[
BFNV19
]
. Define a circuit architecture
A:=\{A_{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
as a family of graphs with
\mathsf{poly}(n)
vertices, where each vertex
v
has
\mathsf{deg}_{\mathsf{in}}(v)=\mathsf{deg}_{\mathsf{out}}(v)\in\{1,2\}
. Intuitively, the vertices of the graph denote one or two qubit gates and the edges denote wires. A quantum circuit is instantiated by specifying the gate for each vertex. Define
{\mathcal{H}}_{A}
as the distribution over circuits formed by drawing a (one or two qubit) gate independently from the Haar measure for each vertex in
A
and assigning the gate to the vertex.

Definition A.1

(Anticoncentration)
.

For an architecture
A
, we say that RCS anticoncentrates on
A
if there exist constants
\kappa,\gamma>0
such that for all large enough
n

\Prr_{C\leftarrow{\mathcal{H}}_{A_{n}}}\left[\mathrm{Pr}_{C}[0^{n}]\geq\frac{1}{\kappa 2^{n}}\right]\geq\gamma

Definition A.2

(Hiding)
.

For an architecture
A
, we say that
{\mathcal{H}}_{A}
has the hiding property if for any
C\leftarrow{\mathcal{H}}_{A_{n}}
and uniformly random
y\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
,
C_{y}
is distributed as
{\mathcal{H}}_{A_{n}}
where
C_{y}
is the circuit such that
\mathrm{Pr}_{C}[x]=\mathrm{Pr}_{C_{y}}[x\oplus y]
, i.e. the circuit
C
with
X
gates appended to every output wire where the value of the output bit in
y
is
1
.

Definition A.3

(Approximate Average-Case Hardness)
.

An architecture
A:=\{A_{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is said to be approximate average-case
\#P
-hard to approximate if it has the following property. There exist functions
\epsilon(n)=1/p(n)
and
\delta(n)=1/q(n)
for some polynomials
p
and
q
such that for any oracle
{\mathcal{O}}
s.t. for all large enough
n

16
16

16

In the literature a slightly different form is often used where
{\mathcal{O}}
takes
1^{1/\epsilon}
and
1^{1/\delta}
as input and must approximate to precision
\epsilon/2^{n}
with error probability at most
\delta
. The version we present is more convenient and cleaner for the purpose of building cryptography, but our results hold for both versions. See the proof of Theorem
5.1
for details.

\Prr_{C\leftarrow{\mathcal{H}}_{A_{n}}}\left[\left|{\mathcal{O}}(C)-\mathrm{Pr}_{C}[0^{n}]\right|\leq\epsilon(n)/2^{n}\right]\geq 1-\delta(n)

it holds that
\mathsf{P}^{\#\mathsf{P}}\subseteq\mathsf{BPP}^{\mathcal{O}}
.

RCS is based on the conjecture that there exists an architecture
A
that satisfies Definition
A.1
, Definition

A.2
, and Definition

A.3
(see, e.g.,
[
BFNV19
]
).
We show that this implies the following conjecture, which directly leads to an instantiatiation of Definition
5.1
.

Conjecture 1
.

There exists an architecture
A
and polynomials
p
and
q
such that:

1.

Anticoncentration.

\Prr_{\begin{subarray}{c}C\leftarrow{\mathcal{H}}_{A_{n}}\\
x\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}\end{subarray}}[\mathrm{Pr}_{C}[x]\geq 1/(p(n)\cdot 2^{n})]\geq 1/\gamma(n)

2.

Hardness.
For any oracle
{\mathcal{O}}
satisfying that for all large enough
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

\Prr_{\begin{subarray}{c}C\leftarrow D_{n}\\
x\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}\end{subarray}}\left[\left|{\mathcal{O}}(C,x)-\mathrm{Pr}_{C}[x]\right|\leq\frac{\mathrm{Pr}_{C}[x]}{p(n)}\right]\geq 1/\gamma(n)-\frac{1}{p(n)}

we have that
\mathsf{P}^{\#\mathsf{P}}\subseteq\mathsf{BPP}^{{\mathcal{O}}}
.

To see why the existence of an architecture satisfying Definition
A.1
, Definition

A.2
, and Definition

A.3
implies Conjecture
1
,
it is first observed that
anticoncentration holds directly from Definition
A.1
and Definition
A.2
. Next, the hiding property implies that approximate average-case hardness holds even for an oracle that takes input
C\leftarrow{\mathcal{H}}_{A_{n}}
and
x\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
and estimates
\mathrm{Pr}_{C}(x)
. Finally, anticoncentration implies that estimating probabilities with small additive error on average implies the ability to estimate probabilities with small relative error on a large fraction of the set of anticoncentrated points.

A.2
Boson Sampling

This section imports conjectures that were made in
[
AA11
]
to obtain quantum advantage from Boson Sampling; and discusses why these conjectures imply Definition
5.1
.

Conjecture 2
.

[Permanent-of-Gaussians-Conjecture]
There exist polynomials
p(\cdot),q(\cdot)
such that for
\epsilon={1}/{p(n)},\delta={1}/{q(n)}
, if there exists an oracle
{\mathcal{O}}
that given as input a matrix
X\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)^{n\times n}_{\mathbb{C}}
of i.i.d. Gaussians, can estimate
\mathsf{Per(X)}
to within error
\pm\epsilon(n)\cdot|\mathsf{Per}(X)|
, with probability at least
1-\delta(n)
over
X
, then

\mathsf{P}^{\#\mathsf{P}}\subseteq\mathsf{BPP}^{{\mathcal{O}}}
.

Conjecture 3
.

[Permanent Anti-Concentration Conjecture]
There exists a polynomial
p(\cdot)
such that for all
n
and
\delta>0
,

\mathrm{Pr}_{X\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)^{n\times n}_{\mathbb{C}}}\Big[|\mathsf{Per}(X)|<\frac{\sqrt{n!}}{p(n,1/\delta)}\Big]<\delta

Theorem A.1
.

Conjectures
2
and
3
imply Definition
5.1
.

Proof.

(Sketched, from
[
AA11
,
GBA+22
]
)
It is shown in
[
AA11
]
how the probability that a randomly chosen linear optical network (i.e. circuit) outputs
0
is proportional to the square of the permanent of an appropriate submatrix of a Haar random matrix; where the submatrix itself is Gaussian. They also prove a hiding property, which argues that any given Gaussian matrix can be embedded into a Haar random matrix while keeping the location of the given submatrix hidden. The embedding procedure itself is known to be in
\mathsf{FBPP}^{\mathsf{NP}}
and is conjectured in
[
AA11
]
to be in
\mathsf{FBPP}
. Subsequent models of Boson Sampling such as (Bipartite) Gaussian Boson Sampling modify the experimental setup and work with Gaussian (as opposed to Haar random) matrices, which allow trivially embedding a Gaussian submatrix in
\mathsf{FBPP}
.

Specifically,
[
GBA+22
]
efficiently (in BPP) reduce the task of computing permanents of Gaussian random matrices to the task of estimating the probabilities of (random) outputs of a bipartite Gaussian boson sampling setup. Under Conjectures
2
and
3
, this proves that the resulting probabilities are
\#\mathsf{P}
hard to approximate, which implies the statement of Definition
5.1
.
∎

A.3
IQP Circuit Sampling

IQP refers to a class of randomly chosen commuting quantum circuits, which take as input the
state
|0^{n}\rangle
, whose gates are diagonal in the Pauli-X basis,
and whose
n
-qubit output is measured in the computational basis. Under
any one of
the two conjectures below, one coming from condensed matter physics and the other from computer science, the output distributions of IQP circuits have been proven classically hard to simulate (unless the polynomial heirarchy collapses)
[
SB09
,
BMS16
]
.

Conjecture 4
.

Consider the partition function of the general Ising model,

Z(\omega)=\sum_{\mathbf{z}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}}\omega\exp\left(\sum_{i<j}w_{ij}z_{i}z_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}v_{k}z_{k}\right),

(18)

where the exponentiated sum is over the complete graph on
n
vertices,
w_{ij}
and
v_{k}
are real edge and vertex weights, and
\omega\in\mathbb{C}
. Let the edge and vertex weights be picked uniformly at random from the set
\{0,\dots,7\}
.

Then it is #P-hard to approximate
\left|Z(e^{i\pi/8})\right|^{2}
up to multiplicative error
1/4+o(1)
for a 1/24 fraction of instances, over the random choice of weights.

Conjecture 5
.

Let
f:\{0,1\}^{n}\to\{0,1\}
be a uniformly random degree-3 polynomial over
\mathbb{F}_{2}
, and define
\text{ngap}(f):=(|\{x:f(x)=0\}|-|\{x:f(x)=1\}|)/2^{n}
. Then it is #P-hard to approximate
\text{ngap}(f)^{2}
up to a multiplicative error of
1/4+o(1)
for a 1/24 fraction of polynomials
f
.

Just like the case of random circuit sampling, IQP circuits satisfy the hiding property trivially due to the circuit architecture supporting the addition of random X gates. Furthermore, the structure of IQP circuits allows provable anti-concentration results for
ngap(f)
and the partition function of the random Ising model. These properties combined help reduce the task of approximating the partition function of the general Ising model, or approximating
ngap(f)^{2}
within the bounds in the conjecture, to the task of approximating output probabilities of randomly chosen IQP circuits. Thus
either one of
Conjectures
4
or
5
implies hardness according to Definition
5.1
.

Appendix B
Flatness of Unitary 2-Design Output Distributions

Theorem
7.2

(Flatness of 2-designs)
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a unitary 2-design on
n
qubits. Fix any
n
qubit state
|\psi\rangle
. For any
C\in\mathsf{Supp}({\mathcal{C}})
, let
p_{C}(x):=|\langle x|C|\psi\rangle|^{2}
be the probability that measuring
C|\psi\rangle
in the computational basis results in
x
. Then the following holds for all
k>6
and sufficiently large
n
. Define

{\mathbb{G}}:=\left\{C\in\mathsf{Supp}({\mathcal{C}}):\underset{x:p_{C}(x)\geq\frac{n^{3k}}{2^{n}}}{\sum}p_{C}(x)\leq 1/n^{k}\right\}

Then

\Prr_{C\leftarrow{\mathcal{C}}}[C\in{\mathbb{G}}]\geq 1-1/n^{k}

Proof.

By the Chebyshev bound applied to random variable
p_{C}(x)
for uniformly random
x
and
C\leftarrow{\mathcal{C}}
, for all
\alpha>0

\displaystyle\Prr_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}\\
C\leftarrow{\mathcal{C}}\end{subarray}}\left[|p_{C}(x)-1/2^{n}|\geq\frac{\alpha}{2^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2n}\left({\mathbb{E}}_{C,x}[p_{C}(x)^{2}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{C,x}[p_{C}(x)]^{2}\right)}{\alpha^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{2n}\left({\mathbb{E}}_{C,x}[p_{C}(x)^{2}]-1/2^{2n}\right)}{\alpha^{2}}

(19)

where the second step follows from the expectation over
x
of
p_{C}(x)
is
1/2^{n}
for all
C
. Since
C
is sampled from a unitary 2-design, we show a bound on the second moment. Fix any
x
. By the definition of
p_{C}(x)
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{C}[p_{C}(x)^{2}]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{C}\left[\left(\langle x|C^{\dagger}|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|C|x\rangle\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{C}\left[\langle x|^{\otimes 2}C^{\otimes 2}|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|^{\otimes 2}C^{\dagger\otimes 2}|x\rangle^{\otimes 2}\right]

\displaystyle=\langle x|^{\otimes 2}{\mathbb{E}}_{C}\left[C^{\otimes 2}|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|^{\otimes 2}C^{\dagger\otimes 2}\right]|x\rangle^{\otimes 2}

By the definition of unitary 2-designs (Definition
4.1
),
{\mathbb{E}}_{C}\left[C^{\otimes 2}|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|^{\otimes 2}C^{\dagger\otimes 2}\right]
equals the second moment operator with respect to the Haar measure applied to
|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|^{\otimes 2}
, i.e.
\underset{U\leftarrow\mu_{H}}{{\mathbb{E}}}\left[U^{\otimes 2}|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|^{\otimes 2}U^{{\dagger}\otimes 2}\right]
which is known to equal
\frac{{\mathbb{I}}+{\mathbb{F}}}{2^{n}(2^{n}+1)}
where
{\mathbb{I}}
is identity and
{\mathbb{F}}
is the flip operator (see Corollary 13 in
[
Mel24
]
). Therefore
